## Claude thinking · 2026-08-16T19:34:42.389Z

There's an existing test at line 171 that asserts the supervisor input prompt should NOT be appended before the ask succeeds, which conflicts with my plan — and another test at line 194 confirms the prompt shouldn't persist if Stop wins during delivery. So the actual intent is to only append the quota-resume prompt after delivery succeeds, not beforehand.

Given that, I shouldn't move `appendSupervisorInputOnDelivery` earlier. Instead, the minimal fix is to mark the worker as "working" (with a CAS on turnGeneration) before calling askAgent, and make sure that status doesn't get stuck if the turn exits without a persisted result. Since "working" is part of `ACTIVE_DIRECT_WORKER_STATUSES`, this should make the run resolve to "running" instead of prematurely "done," preventing the late quota recovery refusal — I'm now checking whether I also need to emit `worker_prompted` beforehand, since that event appears in `COMPLETION_RESET_EVENT_TYPES` in wake.ts.

I'll leave `worker_prompted` firing after the ask as it currently does, since moving it earlier isn't necessary for the fix and could disturb the "delivery succeeded" semantics — the status change alone governs run-status reconciliation. I'm now tracing through the stop/cancel and superseded-CAS paths to confirm they still behave correctly with the status set to "working" beforehand, and starting to think through what happens if askAgent throws a non-quota, non-transient error.

For the transient-error path, though, rethrowing would leave the worker stuck at "working" instead of its prior "idle" state, which could make the run look falsely active even though the incident reopens for a later retry. The cleanest fix is to restore the worker back to "cred-exhausted" on failure, guarded by a CAS check against the current turn generation so it only resets if nothing else has changed the status in the meantime.

Still writing worker turn logic...

Now I need to double-check the abort/stop scenario: when abortWorkerTurn bumps the generation and sets status to "cancelled", the subsequent askAgent rejection triggers my catch block, but the generation-guarded update should no-op since the turnGeneration no longer matches, leaving the cancelled status intact.

I'm also checking whether other code paths emit a named event on worker status changes, and confirming send-message.ts just updates the db without emitting, so I'll stay consistent with that. I'm also verifying the exact literal values for active worker statuses against normalizeWorkerStatus, confirming "working" is correct and matches what send-message.ts uses.

I'm weighing whether to also harden the direct-run-status resolver against races during quota recovery, but deciding the status fix mirroring the established pattern is the real causal fix rather than over-engineering further. Still, I'm tracing a timing window in resumeQuotaExhaustedWorkers where the worker is briefly idle with no incident between resolving the incident and marking it working again, which could let a sweep incorrectly mark it done, though the window is only milliseconds wide.

To close that gap fully, I'm deciding to set the worker status directly to "working" within the same guarded mutation that resolves the incident, computing whether we'll prompt beforehand so the state transition happens atomically rather than in a separate step. I'll keep the activation check inside promptResumedQuotaWorker too, since it's harmless and idempotent even though it's currently only called from this one place.

I'm leaning toward a minimal fix: persist "working" in the resume mutation when we're about to prompt, closing the gap atomically with the incident resolve, and add a try/catch in promptResumedQuotaWorker that restores "cred-exhausted" if askAgent fails. That feels complete without the redundant CAS, though I need to double-check the edge case where the turn gets superseded before askAgent runs.

Now I want to write a regression test confirming the worker stays "working" through the whole resume turn so the sweep can't prematurely mark the run done -- spawning with an idle state, hanging askAgent mid-flight, checking the run status isn't "done" while the call is pending, then releasing it and verifying the response persists correctly.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-16T19:35:03.195Z

Now I understand the test harness fully, so I'm applying two edits to worker-resume.ts: persisting "working" status in the resume mutation when a prompt will follow, and restoring the prior status in promptResumedQuotaWorker if the ask fails. I'm reviewing the mutation logic around the refusal check and status update to wire this in correctly.

I'm adding a comment explaining why the busy status must be set inside the guarded mutation — since the resume turn can run for minutes and an idle worker would let the reconciliation sweep mark the run done prematurely, discarding the agent's work. I'm introducing a `willPromptResumedWorker` flag derived from `shouldPromptResumedWorker` to control whether the status is set to "working" or left as-is, then updating the downstream check to reuse this flag.

For the second edit, in `promptResumedQuotaWorker`, I'm wrapping the `askAgent` call so that if it throws, the worker's status is reset back to `cred-exhausted` — but only if the generation and status still match, so a newer turn or a Stop that already claimed the worker isn't clobbered. This ensures a resume that fails to produce a turn reconciles back to `quota_waiting` instead of leaving the run stuck as `running`. Now I'm second-guessing whether this reopens a quota-related edge case.

Checking the test for a resume that still hits quota exhaustion: askAgent rejects with a quota error, my catch sets the worker to "cred-exhausted", then `handleWorkerQuotaExhaustion` runs and presumably sets the same status again, so that's consistent. For the non-quota resume failure test expecting status "error," my catch sets "cred-exhausted" first but it gets overwritten to "error" afterward by the failureRefusal block, so that's fine too.

I'm confirming `runWorkerTurn`'s callback takes an AbortSignal, so I can just use an async arrow function with no parameters for the askAgent call. I also want to make sure `notifyEventStreamSubscribers()` still fires after the worker status update so the UI reflects "working" correctly — it does, right after the mutation. Time to apply both edits.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-16T19:35:36.004Z

I'm confirming the worker status spread is just for output log purposes, then adding a regression test to worker-resume.test.ts that verifies the worker stays busy throughout the resume turn so the reconciliation sweep can't prematurely mark the run as done while an ask is still in flight.

I verify the worker is marked "working" while the ask is pending, then confirm the mid-turn sweep correctly reports the run as still "running" instead of prematurely closing it. After releasing the ask response, I check that the resume resolves successfully and that the persisted run status never got flipped to "done" during the interruption.

Since `updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` isn't called in promptResumedQuotaWorker, run status should stay "running" for the resume test, which matches expectations. I should also verify the response was actually persisted by checking for the `worker_prompted` event and confirming the outputLog contains it, then write the test and run the suite to make sure my catch block doesn't break existing cases.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-16T19:37:49.502Z

The raw runtime log entries already have the same fields as the unified stream, so they can be appended directly via appendWorkerEntry. I'm planning a one-off recovery script under scripts/ that reads the raw log and appends missing entries, run with tsx like other scripts in the repo.

Since appendWorkerEntry dedups by entry.id, I can safely feed all entries and it'll skip ones already present — but I need to verify the ids actually match between the raw log and unified stream around the quota-limit timestamp, otherwise I'll need to filter by timestamp instead. After appending, I also need to set the run status to something like awaiting_user since the conversation ended waiting on the user's go-ahead, and update the worker's output accordingly.

Checking the counts though: 1704 raw lines minus 443 gives 1261, but the unified stream only has 1115 entries for that same pre-quota period — so compaction or entry-type filtering must be dropping some entries even before the quota hit, meaning a simple id-based replay might not be as clean as I'd hoped.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-16T19:38:07.720Z

The 738 shared IDs confirm overlap, while the 443 post-resume entries are cleanly missing from the unified stream — a good recovery target. The raw-only count of 966 also includes 523 pre-quota entries that were intentionally filtered out during ingestion, so I should only replay the 443 post-resume entries and avoid reinjecting the ones that were deliberately dropped, though I want to double-check whether those 523 were actually meant to be excluded.

Setting that aside since it's not the reported bug, I'll focus on restoring exactly the 443 missing entries. For the recovery script, I'm weighing whether to write a reusable backfill utility in scripts/ (since this class of bug could recur and the repo already has similar utilities like delete-conversations.sh) versus a one-off throwaway script in tmp/ — leaning toward a proper script given the existing conventions. I still need to check the scripts/ directory conventions, the appendWorkerEntry function signature and WorkerEntry requirements, and think through the safety of running this against the live database.

Since appendWorkerEntry uses a file lock, writing to the JSONL while the server runs should be safe at the file level, but the server's in-memory caches could desync if I append from a separate process. I should check whether the server is actually running before deciding whether to do this directly or via an API instead.

```text
1	/**
2	 * Backfill the unified worker conversation stream from legacy
3	 * `.omniharness/agent-runtime-output/<workerId>.jsonl` files written
4	 * by the agent runtime's pre-cutover output archive.
5	 *
6	 * Sessions that ran before the worker conversation stream cutover never
7	 * had their bridge-produced entries (message / thought / tool_call /
8	 * tool_call_update / permission) routed through `appendWorkerEntry`.
9	 * The frontend now reads exclusively from the worker stream, so those
10	 * conversations appear empty in the UI even though the raw bridge
11	 * output is on disk.
12	 *
13	 * This script replays each runtime-output line through
14	 * `appendWorkerEntry`, which is idempotent (dedup by `entry.id`). Safe
15	 * to re-run.
16	 *
17	 * Usage:
18	 *   pnpm tsx scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts
19	 *   pnpm tsx scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts --dry-run
20	 *   pnpm tsx scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts --workerId <id>
21	 *   pnpm tsx scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts --verbose
22	 */
23	import fs from "fs";
24	import path from "path";
25	import readline from "readline";
26	import { db } from "@/server/db";
27	import { workers as workersTable } from "@/server/db/schema";
28	import { appendWorkerEntry } from "@/server/workers/output-store";
29	import type { BridgeWorkerEntryType, WorkerEntry } from "@/server/workers/entries-types";
30	
31	const BRIDGE_TYPES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set<BridgeWorkerEntryType>([
32	  "message",
33	  "thought",
34	  "tool_call",
35	  "tool_call_update",
36	  "permission",
37	]);
38	
39	interface Options {
40	  dryRun: boolean;
41	  workerId: string | null;
42	  verbose: boolean;
43	  runtimeOutputDir: string;
44	}
45	
46	interface FileStats {
47	  workerId: string;
48	  runId: string | null;
49	  totalLines: number;
50	  appended: number;
51	  skippedDuplicate: number;
52	  skippedNonBridge: number;
53	  malformed: number;
54	}
55	
56	function parseOptions(argv: string[]): Options {
57	  const opts: Options = {
58	    dryRun: false,
59	    workerId: null,
60	    verbose: false,
61	    runtimeOutputDir: path.resolve(process.cwd(), ".omniharness/agent-runtime-output"),
62	  };
63	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {
64	    const arg = argv[i];
65	    if (arg === "--dry-run") opts.dryRun = true;
66	    else if (arg === "--verbose") opts.verbose = true;
67	    else if (arg === "--workerId") opts.workerId = argv[++i] ?? null;
68	    else if (arg === "--runtime-output-dir") opts.runtimeOutputDir = path.resolve(argv[++i] ?? "");
69	    else if (arg === "--help" || arg === "-h") {
70	      console.log("Usage: pnpm tsx scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts [--dry-run] [--workerId <id>] [--verbose]");
71	      process.exit(0);
72	    }
73	  }
74	  return opts;
75	}
76	
77	async function processFile(
78	  filePath: string,
79	  workerId: string,
80	  runId: string,
81	  opts: Options,
82	): Promise<FileStats> {
83	  const stats: FileStats = {
84	    workerId,
85	    runId,
86	    totalLines: 0,
87	    appended: 0,
88	    skippedDuplicate: 0,
89	    skippedNonBridge: 0,
90	    malformed: 0,
91	  };
92	
93	  const rl = readline.createInterface({
94	    input: fs.createReadStream(filePath, { encoding: "utf8" }),
95	    crlfDelay: Infinity,
96	  });
97	
98	  for await (const line of rl) {
99	    if (!line.trim()) continue;
100	    stats.totalLines += 1;
101	
102	    let parsed: Record<string, unknown>;
103	    try {
104	      parsed = JSON.parse(line);
105	    } catch {
106	      stats.malformed += 1;
107	      continue;
108	    }
109	
110	    const type = parsed.type;
111	    if (typeof type !== "string" || !BRIDGE_TYPES.has(type)) {
112	      stats.skippedNonBridge += 1;
113	      continue;
114	    }
115	    const id = parsed.id;
116	    if (typeof id !== "string" || !id) {
117	      stats.malformed += 1;
118	      continue;
119	    }
120	    const timestamp = typeof parsed.timestamp === "string" ? parsed.timestamp : new Date().toISOString();
121	    const text = typeof parsed.text === "string" ? parsed.text : "";
122	
123	    if (opts.dryRun) {
124	      stats.appended += 1;
125	      continue;
126	    }
127	
128	    const entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq"> = {
129	      id,
130	      type: type as BridgeWorkerEntryType,
131	      text,
132	      timestamp,
133	      toolCallId: typeof parsed.toolCallId === "string" ? parsed.toolCallId : undefined,
134	      toolKind: typeof parsed.toolKind === "string" ? parsed.toolKind : undefined,
135	      status: typeof parsed.status === "string" ? parsed.status : undefined,
136	      raw: parsed.raw,
137	    };
138	
139	    try {
140	      // appendWorkerEntry dedups by entry.id; replaying is safe.
141	      // We can't observe `appended` from this entrypoint, so we treat
142	      // any non-throwing call as success and post-tally with a count
143	      // delta below. For simplicity here we just count attempts; a
144	      // re-run reports the new dedup hits as `skippedDuplicate=0`
145	      // attempts because dedup happens server-side and is silent.
146	      await appendWorkerEntry(runId, workerId, entry);
147	      stats.appended += 1;
148	    } catch (error) {
149	      stats.malformed += 1;
150	      if (opts.verbose) {
151	        console.warn(`[backfill] ${workerId} line ${stats.totalLines} failed:`, error);
152	      }
153	    }
154	  }
155	
156	  return stats;
157	}
158	
159	async function main() {
160	  const opts = parseOptions(process.argv.slice(2));
161	
162	  if (!fs.existsSync(opts.runtimeOutputDir)) {
163	    console.error(`Runtime output dir not found: ${opts.runtimeOutputDir}`);
164	    process.exit(1);
165	  }
166	
167	  type WorkerRow = typeof workersTable.$inferSelect;
168	  const allWorkers = (await db.select().from(workersTable)) as WorkerRow[];
169	  const workerById = new Map<string, WorkerRow>(allWorkers.map((row) => [row.id, row]));
170	
171	  const files = fs.readdirSync(opts.runtimeOutputDir).filter((name) => name.endsWith(".jsonl"));
172	  const targets = opts.workerId
173	    ? files.filter((name) => name === `${opts.workerId}.jsonl`)
174	    : files;
175	
176	  if (targets.length === 0) {
177	    console.log("No matching runtime-output files.");
178	    return;
179	  }
180	
181	  const totals = {
182	    files: 0,
183	    skippedNoWorkerRow: 0,
184	    totalLines: 0,
185	    appended: 0,
186	    skippedNonBridge: 0,
187	    malformed: 0,
188	  };
189	
190	  for (const file of targets) {
191	    const workerId = file.replace(/\.jsonl$/, "");
192	    const workerRow = workerById.get(workerId);
193	    if (!workerRow) {
194	      totals.skippedNoWorkerRow += 1;
195	      if (opts.verbose) console.log(`[skip] no workers row for ${workerId}`);
196	      continue;
197	    }
198	    const stats = await processFile(
199	      path.join(opts.runtimeOutputDir, file),
200	      workerId,
201	      workerRow.runId,
202	      opts,
203	    );
204	    totals.files += 1;
205	    totals.totalLines += stats.totalLines;
206	    totals.appended += stats.appended;
207	    totals.skippedNonBridge += stats.skippedNonBridge;
208	    totals.malformed += stats.malformed;
209	    if (opts.verbose || stats.malformed > 0) {
210	      console.log(`[done] ${workerId}: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
211	    }
212	  }
213	
214	  console.log("Backfill summary:");
215	  console.log(JSON.stringify({ ...totals, dryRun: opts.dryRun }, null, 2));
216	}
217	
218	main().catch((error) => {
219	  console.error(error);
220	  process.exit(1);
221	});
222
```

This script scoped with `--workerId` filters to the relevant bridge types while excluding metadata like usage and config entries, which is correct since those are just bookkeeping. It dedups by entry.id, so replaying the whole file is safe—shared ids get skipped and only the raw-only entries get appended, though I need to check how the pre-existing entries factor into that count.

I'm worried about ordering though: since seq numbers get assigned sequentially at append time, appending pre-quota entries now would push them after the quota-block entries and break chronological order, so I need to verify whether the reader sorts by seq or timestamp. I also need to check how many of the raw-only entries are actually bridge types versus just usage metadata, and consider whether appending from a separate process while the server is running could cause a conflict given the file locking mechanism.

Beyond that, there's a cross-process caching concern: the running server likely holds its own in-memory seq cache, so appending from this script could create duplicate seq numbers unless that cache revalidates from disk. Stopping and restarting the server would be the safest route, but I want to first check whether the cache refresh logic self-heals based on file mtime or size before deciding that's necessary.

```text
async function refreshChainCaches(runId: string, workerId: string): Promise<{ nextSeq: number; seen: Set<string>; fingerprints: Map<string, string> }> {
  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
  const currentFileState = await readWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);
  const cachedNextSeq = nextSeqByKey.get(key);
  const cachedSeen = seenIdsByKey.get(key);
  const cachedFingerprints = fingerprintsByKey.get(key);
  const cachedFileState = fileStateByKey.get(key);
  if (
    cachedNextSeq !== undefined
    && cachedSeen
    && cachedFingerprints
    && cachedFileState
    && cachedFileState.size === currentFileState.size
    && cachedFileState.mtimeMs === currentFileState.mtimeMs
  ) {
    // Touch the LRU so an actively-used chain stays resident.
    rememberChainKey(key);
    nextSeqByKey.set(key, cachedNextSeq);
    seenIdsByKey.set(key, cachedSeen);
    fingerprintsByKey.set(key, cachedFingerprints);
    fileStateByKey.set(key, cachedFileState);
    return {
      nextSeq: cachedNextSeq,
      seen: cachedSeen,
      fingerprints: cachedFingerprints,
    };
  }

  chainCacheDiskRefreshesForTests += 1;
  // Use RAW persisted entries (pre-dedupe, pre-renumber) for the seq cursor
  // so we never advance backward. A previous in-disk corruption (e.g.
  // duplicate seqs from a cross-process race) used to be "corrected" by
  // normalizeWorkerEntrySeqs renumbering from 1, which then caused the
  // writer to land on seqs already present on disk — making the dupe
  // problem self-reinforcing. Read dedup signals (seen ids, fingerprints)
  // from the canonicalized view, but take maxSeq from the raw stream.
  const rawExisting = await readAllPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
  let rawMaxSeq = 0;
  for (const entry of rawExisting) {
    if (typeof entry.seq === "number" && Number.isFinite(entry.seq) && entry.seq > rawMaxSeq) {
      rawMaxSeq = entry.seq;
    }
  }
  const canonical = normalizePersistedEntries(rawExisting);
  const seen = new Set<string>();
```

## User · 2026-08-16T19:40:17.590Z

Base directory for this skill: /Users/masterman/.claude/skills/learning-from-bugs

# Learning From Bugs

When a bug teaches something reusable, capture the lesson before moving on.

## Use This For

Use this after diagnosing or fixing bugs that involve:

- architecture or ownership boundaries,
- storage, persistence, migrations, caching, or file paths,
- memory management, leaks, lifecycle, cleanup, or resource pressure,
- concurrency, queues, retries, resumability, or recovery,
- control planes, event streams, background workers, or long-running jobs,
- security, data loss, user trust, or incident-like failures,
- any bug that took repeated attempts or revealed a wrong mental model.

Skip it for typos, obvious one-line UI mistakes, throwaway test fixture fixes, or purely local churn that will not help future work.

## Required Output

Write a short learning note to:

`docs/superpowers/learnings/YYYY-MM-DD-<bug-or-lesson-slug>.md`

Create the directory if needed.

Use this shape:

```markdown
# [Lesson Title]

**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
**Context:** [project, feature, or subsystem]
**Symptom:** [what failed]
**Root Cause:** [the real cause, not the symptom]
**Fix:** [what changed]
**Verification:** [commands, tests, or checks that proved it]
**Prevention:** [what future agents should do differently]
**Skill/Doc Updates:** [nearby skills or docs updated, or why no general update was needed]
```

Keep it concrete. Future agents should be able to read the note and avoid the same mistake.

## Update The System When The Lesson Generalizes

If the bug reveals a reusable rule, update the nearest skill or defaults doc in the same change. Examples:

- debugging lesson -> `systematic-debugging`
- verification gap -> `verification-before-completion`
- control-plane or eventing lesson -> `instrumenting-control-planes`
- React lifecycle or routing lesson -> `building-react-apps`
- planning omission -> `writing-plans` or `brainstorming`

Do not bury a general lesson only in a project note. The learning note records the case; the skill update changes future behavior.

## Completion Gate

Before saying a meaningful bug is fixed, check:

1. Is this lesson reusable?
2. Is there a learning note?
3. Did any skill or defaults doc need an update?
4. Did verification prove the fix?

If the answer to 1 is yes, do not skip the note. If the answer to 3 is no, say why in the note.


ARGUMENTS: Quota resume left the worker `idle` for the whole askAgent await, so the direct-run reconciliation sweep marked the run `done` mid-turn and refuseLateQuotaRecovery discarded 15 minutes of completed agent work. Fix: persist "working" in the same guarded mutation that resolves the incident. Run e2168e17ff4b.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01Mx64NaJyv9a9YTDGYGrTec · 2026-08-16T19:40:21.741Z

```text
1	# Quota Recovery Must Own Stale Run Status
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-08-12  
4	**Context:** OmniHarness direct-control quota reset and reload recovery
5	
6	## Symptom
7	
8	A direct run parked for a provider quota reset could lose its recovery dialog after the reset passed while the user was away. On reload, a stale bridge snapshot reported the worker as working, the persisted run became `running` (or remained `failed`), and the durable wake could no longer resume the parked worker.
9	
10	## Root Cause
11	
12	Conversation reconciliation and direct-worker status updates trusted a live-looking worker snapshot without checking whether an open quota incident still owned the run. The quota wake path separately treated a stale `failed` run as terminal. These independent writers could therefore erase or refuse an otherwise valid quota recovery.
13	
14	## Fix
15	
16	Open/recovering quota incidents now retain ownership across reload reconciliation when the durable run/worker state is parked (`quota_waiting` or `cred-exhausted`). Reconciliation reasserts `quota_waiting`, clears stale failure fields, and emits an observable preservation event. A stale quota-owned `failed` run is recoverable by the elapsed-wait sweep, while a genuinely restarted worker still clears disproven recovery bookkeeping.
17	
18	## Verification
19	
20	Added server and lifecycle coverage for stale live snapshots, direct-status writers, stale failed status, lost due wakes, and the complete sleep-through-reset/reload sequence. The focused quota, recovery, and lifecycle suites pass.
21	
22	## Prevention
23	
24	For new server-side state writers, treat durable recovery incidents as ownership records and test competing writers in sequence. Include both sides of the transition: stale snapshots must not erase recovery, while genuinely resumed work must resolve recovery that it disproves.
25	
26	## Skill/Doc Updates
27	
28	No general skill update was needed; this is recorded as a project-specific control-plane learning.
29	

uuid: 0022ce85-bee4-470e-9991-08b891ab71d2
parent: 674cab83-cb94-40c6-9d68-196b089291d4
```

````json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-16-long-awaits-need-a-busy-status-first.md",
  "content": "# Long Awaits Need A Busy Status Before They Start\n\n**Date:** 2026-08-16\n**Context:** OmniHarness direct-control quota reset resume (`src/server/quota/worker-resume.ts`)\n\n## Symptom\n\nRun `e2168e17ff4b` was parked on a Claude session limit at 15:09 with a resume scheduled for 18:00. The wake fired on time, the session reattached, and the agent worked for fifteen minutes: five review passes, a revised plan file, a final answer offering to start Task 1. None of it appeared in the conversation. The run showed as finished at its pre-quota state, and the event log ended with `quota_recovery_refused` — *\"Ignored a late quota recovery callback after the conversation became terminal\"*, `runStatus: done`.\n\nEvery part of the recovery machinery reported success. The parse of \"resets 6pm (Europe/Madrid)\" was right, the wake fired at the right second, `recovery_policy_decision` chose `resume_quota_worker`, and `recovery_resolved` recorded a clean resume. The work was thrown away after all of that succeeded.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n`promptResumedQuotaWorker` awaited `askAgent` before recording anything about the turn. Marking the worker busy, emitting `worker_prompted`, and appending the supervisor input all sat after that await, so for the entire fifteen-minute turn the worker row stayed at `status = idle` with `updated_at` frozen at the resume.\n\n`resolveDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` reads an idle worker as a finished conversation. It is only held off by `ACTIVE_DIRECT_WORKER_STATUSES`, by `cred-exhausted`, or by an active quota incident owning the run. The incident had been resolved two seconds earlier, deliberately, so the \"Waiting for quota reset\" banner would not stay up while the worker was visibly working. That earlier fix removed the last thing pinning `runs.status`. The `quota_wait_preserved` events stop at 18:00:01 and the incident resolves at 18:00:03; from then until 18:15:45 nothing protected the run, and a reconciliation sweep flipped it to `done`.\n\nWhen `askAgent` finally returned, `refuseLateQuotaRecovery` saw a terminal run, returned `ignored`, and dropped the response on the floor. The guard was working exactly as designed. It was reading state that the resume path had itself corrupted.\n\n## Fix\n\nPersist the busy status in the same guarded mutation that resolves the incident, so no window exists between the two:\n\n```ts\nconst willPromptResumedWorker = shouldPromptResumedWorker(resumedWorker.state);\n// ... inside runQuotaRecoveryMutation, same CAS on turnGeneration:\nstatus: willPromptResumedWorker ? \"working\" : resumedWorker.state,\n```\n\n`promptResumedQuotaWorker` now also parks the worker back on `cred-exhausted` if the ask throws, guarded on both `turnGeneration` and the `working` status it set, so a failed resume reconciles to `quota_waiting` instead of stranding the run as `running`, and a newer turn or a Stop keeps its own status.\n\nThe prompt append stays after delivery. That ordering is load-bearing for a different regression (a Stop landing mid-delivery must not leave a phantom `supervisor_input` in the stream) and has its own tests.\n\n## Verification\n\nTwo tests added to `tests/server/quota/worker-resume.test.ts`. The first holds the ask open, asserts the worker reads `working` mid-turn, and runs `updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` at that moment — the sweep that used to end the conversation — asserting it resolves to `running` and that no `quota_recovery_refused` event is recorded. The second asserts a failed ask parks the worker back on `cred-exhausted` and reconciles to `quota_waiting`.\n\nThe first test was confirmed non-vacuous by reverting the one-line status change and watching it fail with `expected 'idle' to be 'working'`. All 18 tests in the quota suite pass, along with the conversations, supervisor, and queued-message suites. One failure in `tests/server/conversations-sync.test.ts:1512` predates this change and reproduces with it stashed.\n\nThe lost turn was recovered: 364 entries replayed from `.omniharness/agent-runtime-output/` into the unified worker stream via `scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts`, filtered to the post-resume window so earlier entries were not appended out of sequence.\n\n## Prevention\n\nAny `await` that owns a worker for the length of an agent turn has to publish the busy status before it starts, not after it returns. `send-message.ts` and `queued-messages.ts` both set `status: \"working\"` inside `runWorkerTurn` before calling `askAgent`; the quota path was the one caller that skipped it, and it was also the path where turns run longest.\n\nThe sharper lesson is about what happens when a protective state is released. Resolving the incident early was correct for the banner, but the incident was doing two jobs: telling the user what was happening, and pinning `runs.status` against reconciliation. Releasing it released both. When you shorten the life of a state that other code reads as ownership, check what else was depending on it, and hand that job to something explicit before you let go.\n\nWatch for the shape where a guard reads state that its own caller is responsible for maintaining. `refuseLateQuotaRecovery` looked correct in isolation and was correct in isolation. Its input was wrong.\n\n## Skill/Doc Updates\n\nNo general skill update. `instrumenting-control-planes` already covers emitting typed events for every decision, and this failure emitted all of them faithfully — the events were how the loss was traced in the first place. The gap was in status ordering around a long await, which is specific to this codebase's worker lifecycle and is now covered by tests next to the code.\n\n## Open Follow-Up\n\nThe same run recorded 3464 `quota_wait_preserved` events between 15:41 and 18:00, roughly one per second. The CAS at `direct-run-status.ts:205` only emits when it actually changes the row, so some other writer was flipping `runs.status` away from `quota_waiting` at that rate for two and a half hours. The `previousStatus: \"running\"` on every one of them names the value being written, not the writer. Not diagnosed.\n"
}
````

## Claude · 2026-08-16T19:41:38.226Z

Done — all three.

## 1. Fixed

`src/server/quota/worker-resume.ts`. The resume path now persists the busy status in the same guarded mutation that resolves the incident, closing the window entirely:

```ts
const willPromptResumedWorker = shouldPromptResumedWorker(resumedWorker.state);
// ...same CAS on turnGeneration that resolves the incident:
status: willPromptResumedWorker ? "working" : resumedWorker.state,
```

`promptResumedQuotaWorker` also parks the worker back on `cred-exhausted` if the ask throws, guarded on both `turnGeneration` and the `working` status it set — so a failed resume returns to `quota_waiting` instead of stranding the run as `running`, and a newer turn or a Stop keeps its own status.

I left the prompt append *after* delivery. That ordering is load-bearing for a different regression (a Stop landing mid-delivery must not leave a phantom `supervisor_input`) and has its own tests.

**Tests:** two added to `tests/server/quota/worker-resume.test.ts`. The first holds the ask open, asserts the worker reads `working` mid-turn, and runs `updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput` at that exact moment — the sweep that killed the run — asserting `running` and no `quota_recovery_refused`. I verified it's non-vacuous by reverting the one-line change and watching it fail with `expected 'idle' to be 'working'`.

18/18 quota tests pass; conversations, supervisor, and queued-message suites pass; typecheck and lint clean. One pre-existing failure at `tests/server/conversations-sync.test.ts:1512` — it reproduces with my change stashed, so it's not from this work.

## 2. Learned

`docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-16-long-awaits-need-a-busy-status-first.md`.

The generalizable part isn't "set the status earlier." It's that resolving the incident early (a deliberate earlier fix, to stop the banner sticking) released a state that was quietly doing two jobs: telling the user what was happening, *and* pinning `runs.status` against reconciliation. Releasing it released both. `refuseLateQuotaRecovery` then looked correct and behaved correctly — on input its own caller had corrupted.

## 3. Recovered

The work product was never lost: `cloudcaptions/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-16-release-gate-e2e-journey.md` (34KB, written 18:13) is on disk and shows as modified in git. Five review passes, Codex verdict READY.

The conversation record was lost, and is now restored. I replayed 364 entries through `scripts/backfill-runtime-output-to-worker-stream.ts` (which already existed for exactly this). I filtered to the post-resume window first — replaying the whole file would have appended 522 older entries at the tail with fresh seq numbers and scrambled the transcript. Verified through the app's own `readWorkerOutputEntries`: 364 entries back, final message intact, seq monotonic, no new duplicates.

Two things worth knowing:

- I corrected `runs.last_activity_at` from 15:09 to 18:15 so the conversation sorts where you'd expect. I did **not** change `status` — `done` is the normal resting state for direct runs (265 of them), so it's already continuable.
- That stream file has 369 duplicate IDs and 4 backward timestamp jumps. Both are identical before and after my backfill, so they're pre-existing and unrelated.

Backup at `tmp/quota-recovery/worker-stream.before.jsonl` if the UI looks wrong. The server was running throughout; appends are file-locked and the seq cache revalidates on size/mtime, but give the conversation a look before I delete the backup.

**Not diagnosed:** the 3464 `quota_wait_preserved` events in 2.5 hours. That CAS only emits when it actually changes the row, so something was flipping `runs.status` off `quota_waiting` roughly once a second. Logged as an open follow-up in the note — want me to chase it?
